They wanted to know if the results confirm the expected phenotype ratios.</p><p>You helped them set up the null hypothesis, so you know that part is correct, but they got confused and were unsure about how to calculate the chi-squared (&chi;&sup2;) value. So much so that they did it three (3) different ways.</p> <p>Before you ask your instructor for a new lab partner, <b>tell them which table is correct AND whether they can reject or fail to reject the null hypothesis</b> using the information provided.</p>	<span style="color: #5d3b00;"><strong>Table 1</strong></span> with &chi;&sup2; = 3.511 is correct and we <span style="color: #e60000;"><strong>REJECT</strong></span> the null hypothesis	Incorrect	<span style="color: #004d99;"><strong>Table 2</strong></span> with &chi;&sup2; = 1.028 is correct and we <span style="color: #e60000;"><strong>REJECT</strong></span> the null hypothesis	Incorrect	<span style="color: #b30077;"><strong>Table 3</strong></span> with &chi;&sup2; = -0.422 is correct and we <span style="color: #e60000;"><strong>REJECT</strong></span> the null hypothesis	Incorrect	<span style="color: #5d3b00;"><strong>Table 1</strong></span> with &chi;&sup2; = 3.511 is correct and we <span style="color: #009900;"><strong>FAIL to REJECT</strong></span> the null hypothesis	Correct	<span style="color: #004d99;"><strong>Table 2</strong></span> with &chi;&sup2; = 1.028 is correct and we <span style="color: #009900;"><strong>FAIL to REJECT</strong></span> the null hypothesis	Incorrect	<span style="color: #b30077;"><strong>Table 3</strong></span> with &chi;&sup2; = -0.422 is correct and we <span style="color: #009900;"><strong>FAIL to REJECT</strong></span> the null hypothesis	Incorrect
